# DeepSeek-V4.1 decode optimization: night log (from 28 Oct 2026, 22:10 UTC)

The user asked me to keep optimizing for at least 10 hours, holding both research locks for the whole series. QFN runs between GPU windows; each window stops it and restores it. Code is on branch `claude/ds41-decode-impl` in `/home/as/radiance-ds41-claude` (local only). Build dir: `/home/as/ds41-claude-build/aql`. Run home: `/home/as/ds41-claude-build/home`, holding plugins from the loop's dev build at `86e71ff9` (same `abi/` as my base).

Workload for every A/B: the loop's 8 held-out prompts, greedy, 64 tokens each, one at a time, TP4. The arguments are the measured run's (80 GiB RAM cache, mapped slabs, SSE2 pack). Each arm starts its own server, so every arm begins with a cold RAM cache. Arms run the same requests in the same order.

| 00:46–00:57 | **Read-ahead of predicted misses** (window ab-20261029T004604Z) | verify-f-M1-Po6: 31,669 rows, 0 differ (read-ahead planes are exact). **Decode: all-f-M1 8.61; top-4 read-ahead 8.37 (−3%); top-6 7.39 (−14%).** A one-layer lead (~2.8 ms) is shorter than a contended read (3+ ms), so misses wait on the in-flight read-ahead anyway (970 of them at top 6). The wasted reads add 30–80% drive traffic and slow every demand read (2.0 → 3.0 ms a weight). **Negative on this hardware:** the drive has no headroom for speculation. Kept in the code, default off. |
| 00:58 | Decode token now (all-f-M1, 115 ms), per rank | 72 ms waiting for the card at the routers (including the all-reduce waiting for other ranks' reads), 19 ms blocked reading missed experts' planes, 21 ms waiting at gathers for the CPU (more threads don't change it, so it is the misses' read latency). |

| 01:00–01:17 | **Per-op GPU profile** (`--profile-ops`; 64- vs 128-token replies to isolate decode) | Decode `moe_gemm_ggml` ~70 µs a call (5.6 ms a token), as in the harness: fine. **Prefill chunks: ~15 ms a call, ~1.23 s of each 2.65 s chunk**; prefill is half of all step time. The harness at the same shape with uniform routing: 1.2 ms (gate_up) + 0.5 ms (down). |
| 01:20 | **Cause found in libr4d's GEMV** (`r4d_ds41_mxfp4.hip`) | The LDS budget holds one whole gate_up activation row (K 5120: 5760 floats of 9216), so an expert with 2+ of a chunk's tokens in a pass took the chunked path. It restages K chunks for every one of the expert's 288 row groups (~1,440 restages and 2,880 barriers a pass). Decode (one row) and uniform routing rarely hit it; real prompts do. **Fix:** whole sub-passes of XLM rows, re-reading the weights instead. Each output keeps its lane and block order, so the bits are unchanged. libr4d rebuilt in 4 min. |

| 01:23–01:29 | **GEMV fix measured** (window ab-20261029T012320Z) | Old vs new plugin on identical inputs, **0 differing output values at every shape**. 16-token gate_up 1145 → 667 µs (uniform routing) and up to 4.6× faster with skewed routing (1589 → 344 µs); down up to 5.2×. Engine, all-f-M1 old vs new plugin: **prefill 5.14 → 6.73 tok/s (+31%), mean TTFT 7.59 → 5.79 s** (warm requests 3.4–5.4 s); decode unchanged (8.83 / 8.74); all 8 texts identical; wall −12%. Committed. |

## Log

- 22:08: took both locks with a long-lived holder (`scratchpad/hold_locks.sh`, released by a file; tooling since moved to `/home/as/ds41-claude-build/work`). Running greedy, base and all.
- 22:10: baseline shutdown report, per rank, over 8 requests:
  - RAM cache: 78% of weight reads hit (32,570 hits, 9,098 misses).
  - The issuing thread spent ~44 s of ~150 s blocked in drive reads and relayout.
  - The links averaged only ~2.4 GB/s.
  - So a token's time is per-layer latency (copies and drive reads each layer waits for), not link bandwidth.
- 22:12: committed the validated integration locally (`53a20415`).
- 22:20: the kernel agent finished; its code is not committed yet.
  - Throughput: 7.0 GB/s cold single thread (was 2.7). At 16 / 20 / 24 threads: 66–68 / 75 / 78–80 GB/s (was 38 / 42 / 43). DRAM-bound from 16 threads.
  - Fast path for scales e∈[113,127], 99.999% of release blocks: weights built as fp16 bits, widened with vcvtph2ps, x pre-halved (exact for every bf16) against 2w, so each product is the same real number as x·w and rounds the same.
  - General path for everything else. `check` passes with new edge cases at -O2/-O3 under GCC 15 and clang 21.
  - Not yet tried: accumulators in local variables, an asm round, FMA (exact here except on under/overflow, so it would need an x guard).
- **22:08–22:21: QFN unintentionally down for ~13 minutes (my error).**
  - What happened: I cancelled a queued window with TaskStop, believing it was still waiting for the locks. It had already taken them and was stopping QFN. The cancel killed it mid-stop. Its restore trap issued `docker start` while the stop was still in flight (a no-op), and the trap's wait was killed too.
  - The next window then waited 10 minutes on a QFN that was not running: an unreachable /stats counted as "busy".
  - Restored at 22:20 (same container, image e8276404…), healthy, answers "Paris. The capital". No requests were lost: the QFN logs showed none in that period.
  - Fixes:
    - The restore ignores further signals and retries `docker start` until the container really runs.
    - Signals lead into the restore.
    - A window refuses to start if QFN is already down.
    - Cancelling goes through a file the script checks between arms.
    - A QFN watchdog (`work/qfn_watchdog.sh`) starts QFN if it is down and no live window owns the stop. It only acts while my lock holder lives.
- 22:22: rebased onto dev head `86e71ff9` (no conflicts; upstream's direct slot fill never lends a filling slot, so it is safe with the CPU path). Rebuilding with the final kernel.
| 01:32–01:40 | **Prefill misses on the CPU, threaded inserts** (ab-20261029T013201Z) | M1 8.72; M16 with 4/8 insert threads 6.22/6.30 — threads did not help. The stats showed why: **3,105 gate_up inserts a rank refused as leased-tail bypasses**. The mover's read windows keep their RAM-cache leases until a window comes round again; with the CPU taking the misses the windows idle, their leased entries drift to the class LRU tail, and every admission behind them is refused. |
| 01:47–01:54 | **Fix: retire the windows' leases after each routed counts sync** (ab-20261029T014721Z, commit 65159c90) | 0 bypasses, 0 leased slots at the end. **M16 decode 8.89 vs M1 8.77 (noise); prefill of the 8 prompts 43.9 s vs 46.3 s (−5%), 7.10 vs 6.74 prompt tok/s.** Texts identical. Same eviction counts in both arms (the CPU-path inserts evolve the cache exactly as the GPU path's direct fill). |
